          Under review. Handshake groups 1 to 11, Spaces groups 12 and 13, a register of every state in group 14, phone layouts in group 15, the phone route in group 16.<br />One Address page, in Management. It never has steps of its own: the server holds the state and the page shows it. Each card is that one page in one state, drawn as desktop content; group 15 draws the long and awkward ones on a phone, and group 16 shows how an owner reaches Address and Handles on a phone and comes back to something under way. Opening either section reads and shows what exists and never starts anything.<br />Handshake (Spec 012, amendments of 2026-09-06 and 2026-09-09): type the name; get one complete update for it; publish it from any Handshake wallet or service, by hand as the normal way; we watch the chain; when everything checks out the owner turns it on. A new connection has no signing step and names no wallet. A connection begun before the change keeps its signing step; that older path is noted in group 1 and kept out of the drawing.<br />Clocks: 14 days to publish; once seen on chain, a day for Handshake to settle it (usually about 8 hours); checks are good for thirty minutes. A missed clock never tears anything down.<br />Things that differ on purpose: an unclear wallet send is not a record mismatch; a mismatch is described from what was just read on chain; trouble before it is on (group 5) is not trouble after it is on (group 6). The address being on, handles being on, requests being open and members' pages working are separate facts (groups 8 and 10); pausing requests leaves issued pages working, and pages can be down while requests are open.<br />Spaces is two separate owner procedures. The address is one pasted signature. Names follow the spec's order: prove; prepare the space once with operate, or skip it if already prepared; wait for Spaces to record it, which is not a timer; prove again under the changed record; confirm the address; hand over; have the handover seen on Bitcoin; then agree the funding terms and open. Preparing does not open requests, confirming the address does not prove the handover, and the handover alone does not open names; only the last step does. Unused fee balance comes back on completing operator exit, not on pausing. No schedule or time is promised anywhere.<br />Reload: the Handshake page and the Spaces address read their state back when opened. Spaces names do not; see the dashed card in group 13.<br />Group 14 gives, per state, the server status, the owner's command, the evidence needed, where it leads including failures, and whether it is built, a candidate, a defect or proposed. Nothing promises a notification: none are wired.<br />Renewal: there is no renewal warning, per the workspace_owner. Keeping the Handshake name renewed is the owner's job; the live card says so once.<br />Unresolved, on purpose: the Spaces commands are illustrative and none is verified against a wallet, version and network, so none can count as executable; ending an open connection has no server command.<br />To draw: the verified Spaces commands, once they exist.
